        if (x1 + x2 + x3 + x4) % 2 == 0:\n                        sites.append([x1, x2, x3, x4])\n    return np.array(sites, dtype=np.float64)\n\n\ndef site_to_index(site: np.ndarray, L: int, site_map: Dict) -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Map a D\u2084 site coordinate to its linear index.\"\"\"\n    key = tuple(site.astype(int))\n    return site_map.get(key, None)\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION II: GRAPH LAPLACIAN AND SPECTRAL ANALYSIS\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass D4GraphLaplacian:\n    \"\"\"\n    The graph Laplacian (interference matrix) of the D\u2084 lattice.\n    \n    For a lattice site n with displacement u_n, the Laplacian encodes\n    the elastic coupling to neighbors:\n    \n        (\u2112u)_n = \u03a3_j (u_n - u_{n+\u03b4_j})\n        \n    where the sum runs over 24 nearest neighbors at positions \u03b4_j.\n    \n    The spectrum of \u2112 determines phonon frequencies via:\n        \u03c9\u00b2 = (J/M*) \u00d7 \u03bb_\u2112\n        \n    where \u03bb_\u2112 are eigenvalues of the Laplacian.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, L: int, periodic: bool = True):\n        \"\"\"\n        Construct the D\u2084 graph Laplacian.\n        \n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        L : int\n            Half-size of simulation box\n        periodic : bool\n            Whether to use periodic boundary conditions\n        \"\"\"\n        self.L = L\n        self.periodic = periodic\n        self.sites = generate_D4_lattice_sites(L)\n        self.N = len(self.sites)\n        \n        # Create site lookup map\n        self.site_map = {tuple(s.astype(int)): i for i, s in enumerate(self.sites)}\n        \n        # Build sparse Laplacian matrix\n        self._build_laplacian()\n        \n    def _wrap_site(self, site: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"Apply periodic boundary conditions.\"\"\"\n        if self.periodic:\n            # Wrap to [-L, L]\n            wrapped = ((site + self.L) % (2*self.L + 1)) - self.L\n            return wrapped\n        return site\n    \n    def _build_laplacian(self):\n        \"\"\"Construct the sparse Laplacian matrix.\"\"\"\n        print(f\"Building D\u2084 Laplacian for {self.N} sites...\")\n        \n        L_matrix = lil_matrix((self.N, self.N), dtype=np.float64)\n        roots = D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_VECTORS\n        \n        for i, site in enumerate(self.sites):\n            neighbor_count = 0\n            for delta in roots:\n                neighbor = site + delta\n                if self.periodic:\n                    neighbor = self._wrap_site(neighbor)\n                \n                j = self.site_map.get(tuple(neighbor.astype(int)), None)\n                if j is not None:\n                    L_matrix[i, j] = -1.0\n                    neighbor_count += 1\n            \n            # Diagonal: degree of vertex\n            L_matrix[i, i] = neighbor_count\n        \n        self.L_sparse = csr_matrix(L_matrix)\n        print(f\"Laplacian constructed: {self.L_sparse.nnz} nonzero entries\")\n        \n    def compute_spectrum(self, n_eigenvalues: int = 100) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the lowest eigenvalues and eigenvectors.\n        \n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        n_eigenvalues : int\n            Number of eigenvalues to compute\n            \n        Returns\n        -------\n        eigenvalues : np.ndarray\n        eigenvectors : np.ndarray\n        \"\"\"\n        print(f\"Computing {n_eigenvalues} lowest eigenvalues...\")\n        \n        # Use shift-invert mode for better convergence on small eigenvalues\n        eigenvalues, eigenvectors = eigsh(\n            self.L_sparse, \n            k=min(n_eigenvalues, self.N - 2),\n            which='SM',  # Smallest magnitude\n            sigma=0.01,  # Shift for stability\n            return_eigenvectors=True\n        )\n        \n        # Sort by eigenvalue\n        idx = np.argsort(eigenvalues)\n        return eigenvalues[idx], eigenvectors[:, idx]\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION III: PHONON DISPERSION RELATION\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass PhononDispersionAnalyzer:\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze phonon dispersion on the D\u2084 lattice.\n    \n    The key prediction: For long wavelengths (small k), the dispersion\n    relation should approach the Lorentz-invariant form:\n    \n        \u03c9\u00b2 = c\u00b2 k\u00b2\n        \n    where c = a\u2080 \u03a9_P is the emergent speed of light.\n    \n    Deviations from isotropy enter only at sixth order due to the\n    spherical 5-design property of D\u2084.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, L: int):\n        self.L = L\n        self.roots = D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_VECTORS\n        \n    def dispersion_relation(self, k: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute \u03c9\u00b2(k) from the lattice dynamical matrix.\n        \n        For a plane wave u_n = A exp(i k\u00b7x_n - i\u03c9t), the equation of motion\n        \n            M* \u2202\u00b2u/\u2202t\u00b2 = J \u03a3_j (u_{n+\u03b4_j} - u_n)\n            \n        gives:\n            -M* \u03c9\u00b2 = J \u03a3_j (e^{ik\u00b7\u03b4_j} - 1)\n            \n        Hence:\n            \u03c9\u00b2 = (J/M*) \u00d7 2 \u03a3_j [1 - cos(k\u00b7\u03b4_j)]\n        \"\"\"\n        # Stiffness ratio (normalized units: J/M* = 1)\n        omega_sq = 0.0\n        for delta in self.roots:\n            k_dot_delta = np.dot(k, delta)\n            omega_sq += 1 - np.cos(k_dot_delta)\n        \n        # Factor of 2 from the standard form\n        return 2 * omega_sq / 24  # Normalized by coordination number\n    \n    def verify_isotropy(self, k_magnitude: float, n_directions: int = 1000) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Verify that \u03c9\u00b2(k) is isotropic for small |k|.\n        \n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        k_magnitude : float\n            Magnitude of the wavevector |k|\n        n_directions : int\n            Number of random directions to sample\n            \n        Returns\n        -------\n        dict with statistics on \u03c9\u00b2 variation across directions\n        \"\"\"\n        omega_sq_values = []\n        \n        for _ in range(n_directions):\n            # Random direction on S\u00b3\n            direction = np.random.randn(4)\n            direction /= np.linalg.norm(direction)\n            \n            k = k_magnitude * direction\n            omega_sq = self.dispersion_relation(k)\n            omega_sq_values.append(omega_sq)\n        \n        omega_sq_values = np.array(omega_sq_values)\n        \n        return {\n            'mean': np.mean(omega_sq_values),\n            'std': np.std(omega_sq_values),\n            'min': np.min(omega_sq_values),\n            'max': np.max(omega_sq_values),\n            'relative_variation': np.std(omega_sq_values) / np.mean(omega_sq_values),\n            'k_magnitude': k_magnitude,\n        }\n    \n    def verify_lorentz_invariance(self, k_max: float = 0.5, n_points: int = 50) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Verify \u03c9\u00b2 \u221d k\u00b2 for small k (Lorentz invariant limit).\n        \n        Returns the fitted c\u00b2 and residuals.\n        \"\"\"\n        k_values = np.linspace(0.01, k_max, n_points)\n        omega_sq_mean = []\n        omega_sq_std = []\n        \n        for k in k_values:\n            result = self.verify_isotropy(k, n_directions=200)\n            omega_sq_mean.append(result['mean'])\n            omega_sq_std.append(result['std'])\n        \n        omega_sq_mean = np.array(omega_sq_mean)\n        omega_sq_std = np.array(omega_sq_std)\n        k_sq = k_values**2\n        \n        # Linear fit: \u03c9\u00b2 = c\u00b2 k\u00b2\n        # Using weighted least squares\n        weights = 1.0 / (omega_sq_std + 1e-10)\n        c_sq_fitted = np.sum(weights * omega_sq_mean * k_sq) / np.sum(weights * k_sq**2)\n        \n        # Residuals\n        predicted = c_sq_fitted * k_sq\n        residuals = omega_sq_mean - predicted\n        relative_error = np.abs(residuals) / omega_sq_mean\n        \n        return {\n            'c_squared': c_sq_fitted,\n            'c': np.sqrt(c_sq_fitted),\n            'k_values': k_values,\n            'omega_sq_mean': omega_sq_mean,\n            'omega_sq_std': omega_sq_std,\n            'residuals': residuals,\n            'max_relative_error': np.max(relative_error),\n            'mean_relative_error': np.mean(relative_error),\n        }\n    \n    def compute_anisotropy_order(self, k_magnitude: float = 0.1) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Determine the order at which anisotropy appears.\n        \n        Due to the spherical 5-design property, the first non-isotropic\n        term in \u03c9\u00b2(k) should be of order k\u2076.\n        \n        We fit: \u03c9\u00b2 = c\u00b2k\u00b2 + \u03be\u2086 k\u2076 P\u2086(\u03b8,\u03c6,\u03c8)\n        \n        where P\u2086 is a sixth-order polynomial in the angles.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Sample at different k magnitudes\n        k_magnitudes = np.logspace(-2, -0.5, 20)\n        relative_variations = []\n        \n        for k_mag in k_magnitudes:\n            result = self.verify_isotropy(k_mag, n_directions=500)\n            relative_variations.append(result['relative_variation'])\n        \n        relative_variations = np.array(relative_variations)\n        \n        # Fit: relative_variation \u221d k^n\n        # log(variation) = n log(k) + const\n        log_k = np.log(k_magnitudes)\n        log_var = np.log(relative_variations + 1e-15)\n        \n        # Filter out very small variations (numerical noise)\n        valid = relative_variations > 1e-10\n        if np.sum(valid) > 3:\n            coeffs = np.polyfit(log_k[valid], log_var[valid], 1)\n            anisotropy_order = coeffs[0]\n        else:\n            anisotropy_order = float('inf')  # Perfectly isotropic\n        \n        return {\n            'k_magnitudes': k_magnitudes,\n            'relative_variations': relative_variations,\n            'fitted_order': anisotropy_order,\n            'expected_order': 4,  # Deviations at k\u2076 \u2192 relative at k\u2074\n        }\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION IV: SPHERICAL 5-DESIGN VERIFICATION\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass SphericalDesignVerifier:\n    \"\"\"\n    Verify that D\u2084 root vectors form a spherical 5-design.\n    \n    A set of points X on S\u00b3 is a spherical t-design if:\n    \n        (1/|X|) \u03a3_{x\u2208X} p(x) = (1/Vol(S\u00b3)) \u222b_{S\u00b3} p(x) d\u03c3\n        \n    for all polynomials p of degree \u2264 t.\n    \n    For D\u2084, t = 5, meaning averaging over the 24 root directions\n    gives the same result as averaging over the continuous 3-sphere\n    for polynomials up to degree 5.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self):\n        # Normalize root vectors to unit length\n        self.roots = D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_VECTORS / D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_NORM\n        self.n_roots = 24\n        \n    def polynomial_average_discrete(self, exponents: Tuple[int, int, int, int]) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute average of x\u2081^e\u2081 x\u2082^e\u2082 x\u2083^e\u2083 x\u2084^e\u2084 over the 24 roots.\n        \"\"\"\n        e1, e2, e3, e4 = exponents\n        values = (self.roots[:, 0]**e1 * self.roots[:, 1]**e2 * \n                  self.roots[:, 2]**e3 * self.roots[:, 3]**e4)\n        return np.mean(values)\n    \n    def polynomial_average_continuous(self, exponents: Tuple[int, int, int, int]) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute average of x\u2081^e\u2081 x\u2082^e\u2082 x\u2083^e\u2083 x\u2084^e\u2084 over continuous S\u00b3.\n        \n        Using the formula for S\u00b3:\n            \u222b_{S\u00b3} x_i^{2n} d\u03c3 / Vol(S\u00b3) = (2n-1)!! / (4 + 2n - 2)!!  \u00d7 (product over dimensions)\n            \n        For odd powers: integral = 0 by symmetry\n        \"\"\"\n        e1, e2, e3, e4 = exponents\n        \n        # Check if any exponent is odd \u2192 integral is zero\n        if any(e % 2 == 1 for e in exponents):\n            return 0.0\n        \n        # For even exponents, use the beta function formula\n        # \u222b_{S\u00b3} x\u2081^{2m\u2081} ... x\u2084^{2m\u2084} d\u03c3 / Vol(S\u00b3) = \n        #   \u0393(m\u2081+1/2)...\u0393(m\u2084+1/2) / \u0393(m\u2081+...+m\u2084+2)\n        \n        from scipy.special import gamma\n        \n        m = [e // 2 for e in exponents]\n        \n        numerator = np.prod([gamma(mi + 0.5) for mi in m])\n        denominator = gamma(sum(m) + 2)\n        \n        return numerator / denominator\n    \n    def verify_design_order(self, max_degree: int = 7) -> Dict[int, float]:\n        \"\"\"\n        Verify spherical design property up to given polynomial degree.\n        \n        Returns maximum absolute error at each degree.\n        \"\"\"\n        errors_by_degree = {}\n        \n        for degree in range(max_degree + 1):\n            max_error = 0.0\n            \n            # Generate all monomials of this degree\n            for e1 in range(degree + 1):\n                for e2 in range(degree - e1 + 1):\n                    for e3 in range(degree - e1 - e2 + 1):\n                        e4 = degree - e1 - e2 - e3\n                        \n                        exponents = (e1, e2, e3, e4)\n                        \n                        discrete = self.polynomial_average_discrete(exponents)\n                        continuous = self.polynomial_average_continuous(exponents)\n                        \n                        error = abs(discrete - continuous)\n                        max_error = max(max_error, error)\n            \n            errors_by_degree[degree] = max_error\n        \n        return errors_by_degree\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION V: BRIDGE METRIC VERIFICATION\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass BridgeMetricVerifier:\n    \"\"\"\n    Verify the continuum limit of the lattice strain \u2192 spacetime metric.\n    \n    The claim: The coarse-grained lattice strain field converges to the\n    smooth metric tensor with error bounded by:\n    \n        ||g_emergent - g_exact|| \u2264 C \u00b7 a\u2080\u00b2 \u00b7 R_max\n        \n    where C = 1/12 for D\u2084 geometry and R_max is the maximum curvature.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, L: int, a0: float = 1.0):\n        \"\"\"\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        L : int\n            Number of lattice sites per dimension\n        a0 : float\n            Lattice spacing (in Planck units, a0 = 1)\n        \"\"\"\n        self.L = L\n        self.a0 = a0\n        self.sites = generate_D4_lattice_sites(L)\n        \n    def impose_strain_field(self, \n                            epsilon_func: callable,\n                            sites: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"\n        Impose a smooth strain field on lattice sites.\n        \n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        epsilon_func : callable\n            Function (x) \u2192 \u03b5_\u03bc\u03bd(x), the strain tensor at position x\n        sites : np.ndarray\n            Lattice site coordinates\n            \n        Returns\n        -------\n        displacements : np.ndarray, shape (N, 4)\n            Displacement field u_n at each site\n        \"\"\"\n        # For small strains: u_i = \u03b5_ij x_j (linear approximation)\n        displacements = np.zeros_like(sites)\n        \n        for i, x in enumerate(sites):\n            epsilon = epsilon_func(x * self.a0)  # Physical coordinates\n            displacements[i] = epsilon @ x * self.a0\n        \n        return displacements\n    \n    def compute_emergent_metric(self,\n                                displacements: np.ndarray,\n                                sites: np.ndarray,\n                                coarse_scale: int = 4) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the emergent metric from coarse-grained displacements.\n        \n        g_\u03bc\u03bd = \u03b7_\u03bc\u03bd + 2\u03b5_\u03bc\u03bd\n        \u03b5_\u03bc\u03bd = (1/2)(\u2202_\u03bc u_\u03bd + \u2202_\u03bd u_\u03bc)\n        \n        Coarse-graining: Average over cells of size (coarse_scale \u00d7 a0)\n        \"\"\"\n        # Compute strain via finite differences\n        # For each site, estimate \u2202u_\u03bc/\u2202x_\u03bd from neighbors\n        \n        N = len(sites)\n        strain = np.zeros((N, 4, 4))\n        \n        # Build neighbor map\n        site_map = {tuple(s.astype(int)): i for i, s in enumerate(sites)}\n        \n        for idx, x in enumerate(sites):\n            for mu in range(4):\n                for nu in range(4):\n                    # Forward difference: \u2202u_\u03bc/\u2202x_\u03bd \u2248 (u_\u03bc(x+e_\u03bd) - u_\u03bc(x)) / a\u2080\n                    x_plus = x.copy()\n                    x_plus[nu] += 1\n                    \n                    # Find neighbor (may need to check parity)\n                    if (sum(x_plus) % 2 == 0):\n                        j = site_map.get(tuple(x_plus.astype(int)), None)\n                        if j is not None:\n                            du_mu = displacements[j, mu] - displacements[idx, mu]\n                            strain[idx, mu, nu] = du_mu / self.a0\n        \n        # Symmetrize: \u03b5_\u03bc\u03bd = (1/2)(\u2202_\u03bc u_\u03bd + \u2202_\u03bd u_\u03bc)\n        strain_sym = 0.5 * (strain + np.transpose(strain, (0, 2, 1)))\n        \n        # Metric: g = \u03b7 + 2\u03b5 (flat Minkowski plus perturbation)\n        eta = np.diag([-1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0])\n        metric = eta + 2 * strain_sym\n        \n        return metric\n    \n    def compute_error_bound(self,\n                            exact_metric_func: callable,\n                            emergent_metric: np.ndarray,\n                            sites: np.ndarray) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the error between emergent and exact metric.\n        \n        Returns\n        -------\n        dict with error statistics\n        \"\"\"\n        errors = []\n        \n        for idx, x in enumerate(sites):\n            g_exact = exact_metric_func(x * self.a0)\n            g_emergent = emergent_metric[idx]\n            \n            # Frobenius norm of difference\n            error = np.linalg.norm(g_emergent - g_exact, 'fro')\n            errors.append(error)\n        \n        errors = np.array(errors)\n        \n        return {\n            'max_error': np.max(errors),\n            'mean_error': np.mean(errors),\n            'std_error': np.std(errors),\n            'errors': errors,\n        }\n    \n    def verify_error_scaling(self, \n                             L_values: List[int],\n                             curvature_scale: float = 0.1) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Verify that error scales as a\u2080\u00b2 for decreasing lattice spacing.\n        \n        For fixed physical size, increasing L decreases a\u2080 \u221d 1/L.\n        Error should scale as (1/L)\u00b2 = 1/L\u00b2.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Define a weak gravitational field\n        def strain_field(x):\n            # Schwarzschild-like at large r: \u03b5 ~ GM/r\n            r = np.linalg.norm(x) + 1e-10\n            magnitude = curvature_scale / r\n            epsilon = magnitude * np.eye(4)\n            epsilon[0, 0] *= -1  # Time-time component\n            return epsilon\n        \n        def exact_metric(x):\n            r = np.linalg.norm(x) + 1e-10\n            magnitude = curvature_scale / r\n            g = np.diag([-1.0 - 2*magnitude, \n                         1.0 + 2*magnitude,\n                         1.0 + 2*magnitude,\n                         1.0 + 2*magnitude])\n            return g\n        \n        results = []\n        for L in L_values:\n            print(f\"Testing L = {L}...\")\n            sites = generate_D4_lattice_sites(L)\n            a0_eff = 1.0 / L  # Effective lattice spacing\n            \n            # Impose strain and compute metric\n            displacements = self.impose_strain_field(strain_field, sites)\n            emergent = self.compute_emergent_metric(displacements, sites)\n            \n            # Compute error\n            error_result = self.compute_error_bound(exact_metric, emergent, sites)\n            error_result['L'] = L\n            error_result['a0_effective'] = a0_eff\n            results.append(error_result)\n        \n        # Fit error scaling: max_error \u221d a\u2080^n \u2192 log(error) = n log(a\u2080) + const\n        log_a0 = np.log([r['a0_effective'] for r in results])\n        log_err = np.log([r['max_error'] + 1e-15 for r in results])\n        \n        if len(log_a0) > 2:\n            coeffs = np.polyfit(log_a0, log_err, 1)\n            scaling_exponent = coeffs[0]\n        else:\n            scaling_exponent = float('nan')\n        \n        return {\n            'results': results,\n            'scaling_exponent': scaling_exponent,\n            'expected_exponent': 2.0,  # Error ~ a\u2080\u00b2\n        }\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION VI: ARO DYNAMICS AND CRITICAL DAMPING\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass ARODynamicsSimulator:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simulate the Axiomatic Reference Oscillator dynamics on D\u2084.\n    \n    The equation of motion with ARO coupling:\n    \n        M* \u2202\u00b2u/\u2202\u03c4\u00b2 + \u03b7 \u2202u/\u2202\u03c4 + J \u03a3_j(u - u_{j}) = \u03bb\u2083 \u03c6_ARO\n        \n    At critical damping (\u03b6 = 1), the response to ARO drive has\n    phase lag \u03c0/2, which generates Lorentzian signature.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, N_sites: int = 100, \n                 J: float = 1.0,\n                 M_star: float = 1.0,\n                 Omega_P: float = 1.0):\n        \"\"\"\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        N_sites : int\n            Number of lattice sites (simplified 1D chain for testing)\n        J : float\n            Spring constant\n        M_star : float\n            Effective mass\n        Omega_P : float\n            ARO frequency\n        \"\"\"\n        self.N = N_sites\n        self.J = J\n        self.M = M_star\n        self.Omega = Omega_P\n        \n        # Natural frequency\n        self.omega_0 = np.sqrt(J / M_star)\n        \n        # Critical damping coefficient\n        self.eta_critical = 2 * np.sqrt(J * M_star)\n        \n        # Damping ratio (set to 1 for critical damping)\n        self.zeta = 1.0\n        self.eta = self.zeta * self.eta_critical\n        \n    def simulate_driven_response(self, \n                                  tau_max: float = 100.0,\n                                  dt: float = 0.01,\n                                  driving_amplitude: float = 1.0) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Simulate driven oscillator response to ARO drive.\n        \n        For a single site driven at frequency \u03a9_P:\n            M* \u00fc + \u03b7 u\u0307 + J u = A cos(\u03a9_P \u03c4)\n            \n        Returns phase lag and amplitude of steady-state response.\n        \"\"\"\n        # State vector: [u, u\u0307]\n        def rhs(tau, y):\n            u, v = y\n            # ARO drive\n            F_ARO = driving_amplitude * np.cos(self.Omega * tau)\n            \n            # Equation of motion\n            dvdt = (F_ARO - self.eta * v - self.J * u) / self.M\n            return [v, dvdt]\n        \n        # Initial conditions (at rest)\n        y0 = [0.0, 0.0]\n        \n        # Integrate\n        tau_span = (0, tau_max)\n        tau_eval = np.arange(0, tau_max, dt)\n        \n        sol = solve_ivp(rhs, tau_span, y0, t_eval=tau_eval, method='RK45')\n        \n        # Extract steady-state response (last half of simulation)\n        n_half = len(sol.t) // 2\n        tau_ss = sol.t[n_half:]\n        u_ss = sol.y[0, n_half:]\n        \n        # Fit to A cos(\u03a9 \u03c4 + \u03c6) to extract amplitude and phase\n        # u(\u03c4) = A cos(\u03a9 \u03c4 + \u03c6) = A cos(\u03c6) cos(\u03a9 \u03c4) - A sin(\u03c6) sin(\u03a9 \u03c4)\n        cos_term = np.cos(self.Omega * tau_ss)\n        sin_term = np.sin(self.Omega * tau_ss)\n        \n        # Least squares fit\n        X = np.column_stack([cos_term, sin_term])\n np.arctan2(A_sin, A_cos)\n        \n        # At critical damping with \u03a9 = \u03c9\u2080, phase lag should be -\u03c0/2\n        return {\n            'amplitude': amplitude,\n            'phase_lag_rad': phase_lag,\n            'phase_lag_deg': np.degrees(phase_lag),\n            'expected_phase_lag': -np.pi/2,\n            'phase_error': abs(phase_lag - (-np.pi/2)),\n            'tau': sol.t,\n            'u': sol.y[0],\n        }\n    \n    def verify_signature_emergence(self) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Verify that the \u03c0/2 phase lag leads to Lorentzian signature.\n        \n        If u(\u03c4) = A cos(\u03a9 \u03c4 - \u03c0/2) = A sin(\u03a9 \u03c4)\n        and we define t via \u03a9 t = \u03a9 \u03c4 - \u03c0/2\n        then u(t) = A cos(\u03a9 t)\n        \n        and \u2202\u00b2u/\u2202\u03c4\u00b2 = -\u03a9\u00b2 A cos(\u03a9 \u03c4 - \u03c0/2) = -\u03a9\u00b2 u\n        \n        In the wave equation, this gives the Lorentzian signature.\n        \"\"\"\n        result = self.simulate_driven_response()\n        \n        # The key check: phase lag \u2248 -\u03c0/2\n        phase_correct = np.abs(result['phase_lag_rad'] - (-np.pi/2)) < 0.1\n        \n        return {\n            'phase_lag': result['phase_lag_rad'],\n            'signature_correct': phase_correct,\n            'interpretation': (\n results['correct_count'] = len(cls.ROOT_VECTORS) == 24\\n        \\n        # Check all norms are \\u221a2\\n        norms = np.linalg.norm(cls.ROOT_VECTORS, axis=1)\\n        results['uniform_norms']                 for neg in negated)\\n        results['closure_negation'] = closure\\n        \\n        return results\\n\\n\\ndef generate_D4_lattice_sites(L: int) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Generate all D\\u2084 lattice sites within a hypercubic region [-L, L]\\u2074.\\n    \\n    Parameters\\n    ----------\\n    L : int\\n        Half-size of the simulation box\\n        \\n    Returns\\n    -------\\n    sites : np.ndarray, shape (N, 4)\\n        Array of D\\u2084 lattice site coordinates\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    sites = []\\n    for x1 in range(-L, L+1):\\n sites.append([x1, x2, x3, x4])\\n    return np.array(sites, dtype=np.float64)\\n\\n\\ndef site_to_index(site: np.ndarray, L: int, site_map: Dict) -> Optional[int]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Map a D\\u2084 =============================================================================\\n\\nclass D4GraphLaplacian:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    The graph Laplacian (interference matrix) of the D\\u2084 lattice.\\n    \\n    For a lattice site n with displacement u_n, the Laplacian encodes\\n    the elastic coupling to neighbors:\\n    \\n        (\\u2112u)_n = \\u03a3_j (u_n - u_{n+\\u03b4_j})\\n        \\n    where the sum runs over 24 nearest neighbors at positions \\u03b4_j.\\n    \\n    The spectrum of \\u2112 determines phonon frequencies via:\\n        \\u03c9\\u00b2 = (J/M*) \\u00d7     Construct the D\\u2084 graph Laplacian.\\n        \\n        Parameters\\n        ----------\\n        L : int\\n            Half-size of simulation box\\n        periodic : bool\\n matrix\\n        self._build_laplacian()\\n        \\n    def _wrap_site(self, site: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Apply periodic boundary conditions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        if self.periodic:\\n            # Wrap to [-L, L]\\n            wrapped = ((site + self.L) % (2*self.L + 1)) - self.L\\n            return wrapped\\n        return site\\n    \\n    def _build_laplacian(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Construct the sparse Laplacian matrix.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        print(f\\\"Building D\\u2084 Laplacian for {self.N} sites...\\\")\\n        \\n        L_matrix = lil_matrix((self.N, self.N), dtype=np.float64)\\n        roots = D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_VECTORS\\n        \\n        for i, site in enumerate(self.sites):\\n            neighbor_count = 0\\n            for delta in roots:\\n                neighbor = site + delta\\n                if self.periodic:\\n                    neighbor = self._wrap_site(neighbor)\\n                \\n                j = self.site_map.get(tuple(neighbor.astype(int)), None)\\n                if j is not None:\\n                    L_matrix[i, j] = -1.0\\n                    neighbor_count += 1\\n            \\n            # Diagonal: degree of vertex\\n            L_matrix[i, i] = neighbor_count\\n        \\n        self.L_sparse = csr_matrix(L_matrix)\\n        print(f\\\"Laplacian constructed: {self.L_sparse.nnz} nonzero entries\\\")\\n        \\n    def compute_spectrum(self, n_eigenvalues: int = 100) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Compute the lowest   -------\\n        eigenvalues : np.ndarray\\n        eigenvectors : np.ndarray\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        print(f\\\"Computing {n_eigenvalues} lowest eigenvalues...\\\")\\n        \\n        # DISPERSION RELATION\\n# =============================================================================\\n\\nclass PhononDispersionAnalyzer:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Analyze phonon dispersion on the D\\u2084 lattice.\\n    \\n    The key prediction: For long wavelengths (small k), the dispersion\\n    relation should approach the Lorentz-invariant form:\\n    \\n        \\u03c9\\u00b2 = c\\u00b2 k\\u00b2\\n        \\n    where c = a\\u2080 \\u03a9_P is the emergent speed of light.\\n    \\n    Deviations from isotropy enter only at sixth order due to the\\n    spherical 5-design property of D\\u2084.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    \\n    def __init__(self, L: int):\\n        self.L = L\\n        self.roots = D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_VECTORS\\n        \\n    def dispersion_relation(self, k: np.ndarray) -> float:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Compute \\u03c9\\u00b2(k) from the lattice dynamical matrix.\\n        \\n        For a plane wave u_n = A exp(i k\\u00b7x_n - i\\u03c9t), the equation of motion\\n        \\n            M* \\u2202\\u00b2u/\\u2202t\\u00b2 = J \\u03a3_j (u_{n+\\u03b4_j} - u_n)\\n            \\n        gives:\\n            -M* \\u03c9\\u00b2 = J \\u03a3_j (normalized units: J/M* = 1)\\n        omega_sq = 0.0\\n        for delta in self.roots:\\n            k_dot_delta = np.dot(k, delta)\\n            omega_sq += 1 - np.cos(k_dot_delta)\\n        \\n        # Factor of 2 from the standard form\\n        return 2 * omega_sq / 24  # Normalized by coordination number\\n    \\n    def verify_isotropy(self, k_magnitude: float, n_directions: int = 1000) -> Dict:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Verify that \\u03c9\\u00b2(k) is isotropic for small |k|.\\n        \\n        Parameters\\n        ----------\\n        k_magnitude : float\\n            Magnitude of the wavevector |k|\\n        n_directions : int\\n            Number of random directions to sample\\n            \\n        Returns\\n        -------\\n        dict with statistics on \\u03c9\\u00b2 variation across directions\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        omega_sq_values = []\\n        \\n        for _ in range(n_directions):\\n            # Random direction on S\\u00b3\\n            direction = np.random.randn(4)\\n            direction /= np.linalg.norm(direction)\\n            \\n            k = k_magnitude * direction\\n            omega_sq = np.std(omega_sq_values) / np.mean(omega_sq_values),\\n            'k_magnitude': k_magnitude,\\n        }\\n    \\n    def verify_lorentz_invariance(self, k_max: float = 0.5, n_points: int = 50) -> Dict:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Verify \\u03c9\\u00b2 \\u221d k\\u00b2 for small k (Lorentz invariant limit).\\n        \\n        Returns the fitted c\\u00b2 and residuals.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        k_values = np.linspace(0.01, k_max, n_points)\\n        omega_sq_mean = []\\n        omega_sq_std = []\\n        \\n        for k in k_values:\\n            result = self.verify_isotropy(k, n_directions=200)\\n            omega_sq_mean.append(result['mean'])\\n            omega_sq_std.append(result['std'])\\n        \\n        omega_sq_mean = np.array(omega_sq_mean)\\n        omega_sq_std = np.array(omega_sq_std)\\n        k_sq = k_values**2\\n        \\n        # Linear fit: \\u03c9\\u00b2 = c\\u00b2 k\\u00b2\\n        # Using weighted least squares\\n        weights = 1.0 / (omega_sq_std + 1e-10)\\n        c_sq_fitted = np.sum(weights * omega_sq_mean * k_sq) / np.sum(weights * k_sq**2)\\n        \\n        # Residuals\\n        predicted = c_sq_fitted * k_sq\\n        residuals = omega_sq_mean - predicted\\n        relative_error = np.abs(residuals) / omega_sq_mean\\n        \\n        return {\\n            'c_squared': c_sq_fitted,\\n            'c': np.sqrt(c_sq_fitted),\\n            'k_values': k_values,\\n            'omega_sq_mean': omega_sq_mean,\\n            'omega_sq_std': omega_sq_std,\\n            'residuals': residuals,\\n            'max_relative_error': np.max(relative_error),\\n            'mean_relative_error': np.mean(relative_error),\\n        }\\n    \\n    def compute_anisotropy_order(self, k_magnitude: float = 0.1) -> Dict:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Determine the order at which anisotropy appears.\\n        \\n        Due to the spherical 5-design property, the first non-isotropic\\n        term in \\u03c9\\u00b2(k) should be of order k\\u2076.\\n        \\n        We fit: \\u03c9\\u00b2 = c\\u00b2k\\u00b2 + \\u03be\\u2086 k\\u2076 P\\u2086(\\u03b8,\\u03c6,\\u03c8)\\n        \\n        where P\\u2086 is a sixth-order polynomial in the angles.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Sample at different k magnitudes\\n        k_magnitudes = np.logspace(-2, -0.5, 20)\\n        relative_variations = []\\n        \\n        for k_mag in k_magnitudes:\\n            result = self.verify_isotropy(k_mag, n_directions=500)\\n            relative_variations.append(result['relative_variation'])\\n        \\n        relative_variations = np.array(relative_variations)\\n        \\n        # Fit: relative_variation \\u221d k^n\\n        # log(variation) = n log(k) + const\\n        log_k = np.log(k_magnitudes)\\n        log_var = np.log(relative_variations + 1e-15)\\n        \\n        # Filter out very small variations (numerical noise)\\n        valid = relative_variations > 1e-10\\n        if np.sum(valid) > 3:\\n            coeffs = np.polyfit(log_k[valid], log_var[valid], 1)\\n            anisotropy_order = coeffs[0]\\n        else:\\n            anisotropy_order = float('inf')  # Perfectly isotropic\\n        \\n        return {\\n            'k_magnitudes': k_magnitudes,\\n            'relative_variations': relative_variations,\\n            'fitted_order': anisotropy_order,\\n            'expected_order': 4,  # Deviations at k\\u2076 \\u2192 relative at k\\u2074\\n        }\\n\\n\\n# =============================================================================\\n# SECTION IV: SPHERICAL 5-DESIGN VERIFICATION\\n# =============================================================================\\n\\nclass SphericalDesignVerifier:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Verify that D\\u2084 root vectors form a spherical 5-design.\\n    \\n    A set of points X on S\\u00b3 is a spherical t-design if:\\n    \\n        (1/|X|) \\u03a3_{x\\u2208X} p(x) = (1/Vol(S\\u00b3)) \\u222b_{S\\u00b3} p(x) d\\u03c3\\n        \\n    for all polynomials p of degree \\u2264 t.\\n    \\n    For D\\u2084, t = 5, meaning averaging over the 24 root directions\\n    gives the same result as averaging over the continuous 3-sphere\\n    for polynomials up to degree 5.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    \\n    def __init__(self):\\n        # Normalize root vectors to unit length\\n        self.roots = D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_VECTORS / D4LatticeGeometry.ROOT_NORM\\n        self.n_roots = 24\\n        \\n    def polynomial_average_discrete(self, exponents: Tuple[int, int, int, int]) -> float:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Compute average of x\\u2081^e\\u2081 x\\u2082^e\\u2082 x\\u2083^e\\u2083 x\\u2084^e\\u2084 over the 24 roots.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        e1, e2, e3, e4 = exponents\\n        values = (self.roots[:, 0]**e1 * self.roots[:, 1]**e2 * \\n                  self.roots[:, 2]**e3 * self.roots[:, 3]**e4)\\n        return np.mean(values)\\n    \\n    def polynomial_average_continuous(self, exponents: Tuple[int, int, int, int]) -> float:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Compute average of x\\u2081^e\\u2081 x\\u2082^e\\u2082 x\\u2083^e\\u2083 x\\u2084^e\\u2084 over continuous S\\u00b3.\\n        \\n        Using the formula for S\\u00b3:\\n            \\u222b_{S\\u00b3} x_i^{2n} d\\u03c3 / Vol(S\\u00b3) = (2n-1)!! / (4 + 2n - 2)!!  \\u00d7 (product over dimensions)\\n            \\n        For odd powers: integral = 0 by symmetry\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        e1, e2, e3, e4 = exponents\\n        \\n        # Check if any exponent is odd \\u2192 integral is zero\\n        if any(e % 2 == 1 for e in exponents):\\n            return 0.0\\n        \\n        # For even exponents, use the beta function formula\\n        # \\u222b_{S\\u00b3} x\\u2081^{2m\\u2081} ... x\\u2084^{2m\\u2084} d\\u03c3 / Vol(S\\u00b3) = \\n        #   \\u0393(m\\u2081+1/2)...\\u0393(m\\u2084+1/2) / \\u0393(m\\u2081+...+m\\u2084+2)\\n        \\n        from scipy.special import gamma\\n        \\n        m = [e // 2 for e in exponents]\\n        \\n        numerator = np.prod([gamma(mi + 0.5) for mi in m])\\n        denominator = gamma(sum(m) + 2)\\n        \\n        return numerator / denominator\\n    \\n    def verify_design_order(self, max_degree: int = 7) -> Dict[int, float]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Verify spherical design property up to given polynomial degree.\\n        \\n        Returns maximum absolute error at each degree.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        errors_by_degree = {}\\n        \\n        for degree in range(max_degree + 1):\\n            max_error = 0.0\\n            \\n            # Generate all monomials of this degree\\n            for e1 in range(degree + 1):\\n                for e2 in range(degree - e1 + 1):\\n                    for e3 in range(degree - e1 - e2 + 1):\\n                        e4 = degree - e1 - e2 - e3\\n                        \\n                        exponents = (e1, e2, e3, e4)\\n                        \\n                        discrete = self.polynomial_average_discrete(exponents)\\n                        continuous = self.polynomial_average_continuous(exponents)\\n                        \\n                        error = abs(discrete - continuous)\\n                        max_error = max(max_error, error)\\n            \\n            errors_by_degree[degree] = max_error\\n        \\n        return errors_by_degree\\n\\n\\n# =============================================================================\\n# SECTION V: BRIDGE METRIC VERIFICATION\\n# =============================================================================\\n\\nclass BridgeMetricVerifier:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Verify the continuum limit of the lattice strain \\u2192 spacetime metric.\\n    \\n    The claim: The coarse-grained lattice strain field converges to the\\n    smooth metric tensor with error bounded by:\\n    \\n        ||g_emergent - g_exact|| \\u2264 C \\u00b7 a\\u2080\\u00b2 \\u00b7 R_max\\n        \\n    where C = 1/12 for D\\u2084 geometry and R_max is the maximum curvature.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    \\n    def __init__(self, L: int, a0: float = 1.0):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Parameters\\n        ----------\\n        L : int\\n            Number of lattice sites per dimension\\n        a0 : float\\n            Lattice spacing (in Planck units, a0 = 1)\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.L = L\\n        self.a0 = a0\\n        self.sites = generate_D4_lattice_sites(L)\\n        \\n    def impose_strain_field(self, \\n                            epsilon_func: callable,\\n                            sites: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Impose a smooth strain field on lattice sites.\\n        \\n        Parameters\\n        ----------\\n        epsilon_func : callable\\n            Function (x) \\u2192 \\u03b5_\\u03bc\\u03bd(x), the strain tensor at position x\\n        sites : np.ndarray\\n            Lattice site coordinates\\n            \\n        Returns\\n        -------\\n        displacements : np.ndarray, shape (N, 4)\\n            Displacement field u_n at each site\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # For small strains: u_i = \\u03b5_ij x_j (linear approximation)\\n        displacements = np.zeros_like(sites)\\n        \\n        for i, x in enumerate(sites):\\n            epsilon = epsilon_func(x * self.a0)  # Physical coordinates\\n            displacements[i] = epsilon @ x * self.a0\\n        \\n        return displacements\\n    \\n    def compute_emergent_metric(self,\\n                                displacements: np.ndarray,\\n                                sites: np.ndarray,\\n                                coarse_scale: int = 4) -> np.ndarray:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Compute the emergent metric from coarse-grained displacements.\\n        \\n        g_\\u03bc\\u03bd = \\u03b7_\\u03bc\\u03bd + 2\\u03b5_\\u03bc\\u03bd\\n        \\u03b5_\\u03bc\\u03bd = (1/2)(\\u2202_\\u03bc u_\\u03bd + \\u2202_\\u03bd u_\\u03bc)\\n        \\n        Coarse-graining: Average over cells of size (coarse_scale \\u00d7 a0)\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Compute strain via finite differences\\n        # For each site, estimate \\u2202u_\\u03bc/\\u2202x_\\u03bd from neighbors\\n        \\n        N = len(sites)\\n        strain = np.zeros((N, 4, 4))\\n        \\n        # Build neighbor map\\n        site_map = {tuple(s.astype(int)): i for i, s in enumerate(sites)}\\n        \\n        for idx, x in enumerate(sites):\\n            for mu in range(4):\\n                for nu in range(4):\\n                    # Forward difference: \\u2202u_\\u03bc/\\u2202x_\\u03bd \\u2248 (u_\\u03bc(x+e_\\u03bd) - u_\\u03bc(x)) / a\\u2080\\n                    x_plus = x.copy()\\n                    x_plus[nu] += 1\\n                    \\n                    # Find neighbor (may need to check parity)\\n                    if (sum(x_plus) % 2 == 0):\\n                        j = site_map.get(tuple(x_plus.astype(int)), None)\\n                        if j is not None:\\n                            du_mu = displacements[j, mu] - displacements[idx, mu]\\n                            strain[idx, mu, nu] = du_mu / self.a0\\n        \\n        # Symmetrize: \\u03b5_\\u03bc\\u03bd = (1/2)(\\u2202_\\u03bc u_\\u03bd + \\u2202_\\u03bd u_\\u03bc)\\n        strain_sym = 0.5 * (strain + np.transpose(strain, (0, 2, 1)))\\n        \\n        # Metric: g = \\u03b7 + 2\\u03b5 (flat Minkowski plus perturbation)\\n        eta = np.diag([-1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0])\\n        metric = eta + 2 * strain_sym\\n        \\n        return metric\\n    \\n    def compute_error_bound(self,\\n                            exact_metric_func: callable,\\n                            emergent_metric: np.ndarray,\\n                            sites: np.ndarray) -> Dict:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Compute the error between emergent and exact metric.\\n        \\n        Returns\\n        -------\\n        dict with error statistics\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        errors = []\\n        \\n        for idx, x in enumerate(sites):\\n            g_exact = exact_metric_func(x * self.a0)\\n            g_emergent = emergent_metric[idx]\\n            \\n            # Frobenius norm of difference\\n            error = np.linalg.norm(g_emergent - g_exact, 'fro')\\n            errors.append(error)\\n        \\n        errors = np.array(errors)\\n        \\n        return {\\n            'max_error': np.max(errors),\\n            'mean_error': np.mean(errors),\\n            'std_error': np.std(errors),\\n            'errors': errors,\\n        }\\n    \\n    def verify_error_scaling(self, \\n                             L_values: List[int],\\n                             curvature_scale: float = 0.1) -> Dict:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Verify that error scales as a\\u2080\\u00b2 for decreasing lattice spacing.\\n        \\n        For fixed physical size, increasing L decreases a\\u2080 \\u221d 1/L.\\n        Error should scale as (1/L)\\u00b2 = 1/L\\u00b2.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # Define a weak gravitational field\\n        def strain_field(x):\\n            # Schwarzschild-like at large r: \\u03b5 ~ GM/r\\n            r = np.linalg.norm(x) + 1e-10\\n            magnitude = curvature_scale / r\\n            epsilon = magnitude * np.eye(4)\\n            epsilon[0, 0] *= -1  # Time-time component\\n            return epsilon\\n        \\n        def exact_metric(x):\\n            r = np.linalg.norm(x) + 1e-10\\n            magnitude = curvature_scale / r\\n            g = np.diag([-1.0 - 2*magnitude, \\n                         1.0 + 2*magnitude,\\n                         1.0 + 2*magnitude,\\n                         1.0 + 2*magnitude])\\n            return g\\n        \\n        results = []\\n        for L in L_values:\\n            print(f\\\"Testing L = {L}...\\\")\\n            sites = generate_D4_lattice_sites(L)\\n            a0_eff = 1.0 / L  # Effective lattice spacing\\n            \\n            # Impose strain and compute metric\\n            displacements = self.impose_strain_field(strain_field, sites)\\n            emergent = self.compute_emergent_metric(displacements, sites)\\n            \\n            # Compute error\\n            error_result = self.compute_error_bound(exact_metric, emergent, sites)\\n            error_result['L'] = L\\n            error_result['a0_effective'] = a0_eff\\n            results.append(error_result)\\n        \\n        # Fit error scaling: max_error \\u221d a\\u2080^n \\u2192 log(error) = n log(a\\u2080) + const\\n        log_a0 = np.log([r['a0_effective'] for r in results])\\n        log_err = np.log([r['max_error'] + 1e-15 for r in results])\\n        \\n        if len(log_a0) > 2:\\n            coeffs = np.polyfit(log_a0, log_err, 1)\\n            scaling_exponent = coeffs[0]\\n        else:\\n            scaling_exponent = float('nan')\\n        \\n        return {\\n            'results': results,\\n            'scaling_exponent': scaling_exponent,\\n            'expected_exponent': 2.0,  # Error ~ a\\u2080\\u00b2\\n        }\\n\\n\\n# =============================================================================\\n# SECTION VI: ARO DYNAMICS AND CRITICAL DAMPING\\n# =============================================================================\\n\\nclass ARODynamicsSimulator:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Simulate the Axiomatic Reference Oscillator dynamics on D\\u2084.\\n    \\n    The equation of motion with ARO coupling:\\n    \\n        M* \\u2202\\u00b2u/\\u2202\\u03c4\\u00b2 + \\u03b7 \\u2202u/\\u2202\\u03c4 + J \\u03a3_j(u - u_{j}) = \\u03bb\\u2083 \\u03c6_ARO\\n        \\n    At critical damping (\\u03b6 = 1), the response to ARO drive has\\n    phase lag \\u03c0/2, which generates Lorentzian signature.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    \\n    def __init__(self, N_sites: int = 100, \\n                 J: float = 1.0,\\n                 M_star: float = 1.0,\\n                 Omega_P: float = 1.0):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Parameters\\n        ----------\\n        N_sites : int\\n            Number of lattice sites (simplified 1D chain for testing)\\n        J : float\\n            Spring constant\\n        M_star : float\\n            Effective mass\\n        Omega_P : float\\n            ARO frequency\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        self.N = N_sites\\n        self.J = J\\n        self.M = M_star\\n        self.Omega = Omega_P\\n        \\n        # Natural frequency\\n        self.omega_0 = np.sqrt(J / M_star)\\n        \\n        # Critical damping coefficient\\n        self.eta_critical = 2 * np.sqrt(J * M_star)\\n        \\n        # Damping ratio (set to 1 for critical damping)\\n        self.zeta = 1.0\\n        self.eta = self.zeta * self.eta_critical\\n        \\n    def simulate_driven_response(self, \\n                                  tau_max: float = 100.0,\\n                                  dt: float = 0.01,\\n                                  driving_amplitude: float = 1.0) -> Dict:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Simulate driven oscillator response to ARO drive.\\n        \\n        For a single site driven at frequency \\u03a9_P:\\n            M* \\u00fc + \\u03b7 u\\u0307 + J u = A cos(\\u03a9_P \\u03c4)\\n            \\n        Returns phase lag and amplitude of steady-state response.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        # State vector: [u, u\\u0307]\\n        def rhs(tau, y):\\n            u, v = y\\n            # ARO drive\\n            F_ARO = driving_amplitude * np.cos(self.Omega * tau)\\n            \\n            # Equation of motion\\n            dvdt = (F_ARO - self.eta * v - self.J * u) / self.M\\n            return [v, dvdt]\\n        \\n        # Initial conditions (at rest)\\n        y0 = [0.0, 0.0]\\n        \\n        # Integrate\\n        tau_span = (0, tau_max)\\n        tau_eval = np.arange(0, tau_max, dt)\\n        \\n        sol = solve_ivp(rhs, tau_span, y0, t_eval=tau_eval, method='RK45')\\n        \\n        # Extract steady-state response (last half of simulation)\\n        n_half = len(sol.t) // 2\\n        tau_ss = sol.t[n_half:]\\n        u_ss = sol.y[0, n_half:]\\n        \\n        # Fit to A cos(\\u03a9 \\u03c4 + \\u03c6) to extract amplitude and phase\\n        # u(\\u03c4) = A cos(\\u03a9 \\u03c4 + \\u03c6) = A cos(\\u03c6) cos(\\u03a9 \\u03c4) - A sin(\\u03c6) sin(\\u03a9 \\u03c4)\\n        cos_term = np.cos(self.Omega * tau_ss)\\n        sin_term = np.sin(self.Omega * tau_ss)\\n        \\n        # Least squares fit\\n        X = np.column_stack([cos_term, sin_term])\\n        coeffs, _, _, _ = np.linalg.lstsq(X, u_ss, rcond=None)\\n        \\n        A_cos, A_sin = coeffs[0], -coeffs[1]\\n        amplitude = np.sqrt(A_cos**2 + A_sin**2)\\n        phase_lag = np.arctan2(A_sin, A_cos)\\n        \\n        # At critical damping with \\u03a9 = \\u03c9\\u2080, phase lag should be -\\u03c0/2\\n        return {\\n            'amplitude': amplitude,\\n            'phase_lag_rad': phase_lag,\\n            'phase_lag_deg': np.degrees(phase_lag),\\n            'expected_phase_lag': -np.pi/2,\\n            'phase_error': abs(phase_lag - (-np.pi/2)),\\n            'tau': sol.t,\\n            'u': sol.y[0],\\n        }\\n    \\n    def verify_signature_emergence(self) -> Dict:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Verify that the \\u03c0/2 phase lag leads to Lorentzian signature.\\n        \\n        If u(\\u03c4) = A cos(\\u03a9 \\u03c4 - \\u03c0/2) = A sin(\\u03a9 \\u03c4)\\n        and we define t via \\u03a9 t = \\u03a9 \\u03c4 - \\u03c0/2\\n        then u(t) = A cos(\\u03a9 t)\\n        \\n np.abs(result['phase_lag_rad'] - (-np.pi/2)) < 0.1\\n        \\n        return {\\n            'phase_lag': result['phase_lag_rad'],\\n            'signature_correct': phase_correct,\\n       "file_text": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\n=============================================================================\nIRH v72.0 \u2014 Planck-Scale Scattering Amplitudes on the D\u2084 Lattice\n=============================================================================\n\nThis module develops the S-matrix formalism for particle scattering on the\nD\u2084 cymatic resonance network, with particular focus on:\n\n1. Vertex factors from the unified lattice action\n2. Propagator structure including lattice corrections\n3. Tree-level amplitudes for triality braid scattering  \n4. Unitarity and causality verification\n5. Connection to Standard Model amplitudes in the continuum limit\n\nThe key insight: At Planck energies, the discreteness of the D\u2084 lattice\nbecomes manifest, and amplitudes deviate from the continuum QFT predictions.\nThese deviations are specific and calculable, providing falsifiable predictions.\n\nAuthor: Brandon D. McCrary\nDate: February 2026\nVersion: 72.0\n=============================================================================\n\"\"\"\n\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.special import gamma, factorial\nfrom typing import Tuple, List, Dict, Optional, Callable\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom enum import Enum\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n# Physical constants (Planck units: \u210f = c = G = 1)\nPLANCK_MASS = 1.0\nPLANCK_LENGTH = 1.0\nALPHA_EM = 1.0 / 137.036\nALPHA_S = 0.118\n\n# D\u2084 lattice parameters\nCOORDINATION_NUMBER = 24\nDIM_SO8 = 28\nDIM_G2 = 14\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION I: TRIALITY REPRESENTATION STRUCTURE\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass TrialityBranch(Enum):\n    \"\"\"The three branches of D\u2084 triality.\"\"\"\n    VECTOR = 0      # 8_v representation\n    SPINOR_PLUS = 1  # 8_s representation  \n    SPINOR_MINUS = 2 # 8_c representation\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass TrialityBraid:\n    \"\"\"\n    Representation of a particle as a triality braid defect.\n    \n    A triality braid is characterized by:\n    - branch: Which triality representation it transforms under\n    - winding: Integer winding number (topological charge)\n    - phase: The Koide phase angle \u03b8\n    - generation: 0, 1, 2 (for electron, muon, tau)\n    \"\"\"\n    branch: TrialityBranch\n    winding: int\n    phase: float\n    generation: int\n    \n    @property\n    def mass(self) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute mass from the Koide formula.\n        \n        m_n = M_scale \u00d7 [1 + \u221a2 cos(\u03b8\u2080 + 2\u03c0n/3)]\u00b2\n        \"\"\"\n        theta_0 = 2.0 / 9.0  # Fundamental Koide angle\n        theta_n = theta_0 + 2 * np.pi * self.generation / 3\n        amplitude = 1 + np.sqrt(2) * np.cos(theta_n)\n        \n        # M_scale calibrated to electron mass\n        M_scale = 0.511e-3 / (1 + np.sqrt(2) * np.cos(theta_0))**2\n        return M_scale * amplitude**2\n    \n    @property\n    def electric_charge(self) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Electric charge from triality representation.\n        \n        Leptons (closed braids): Q = \u00b11\n        Quarks (open braids): Q = \u00b11/3, \u00b12/3\n        \"\"\"\n        if self.winding == 1:  # Lepton\n            return -1.0\n        elif self.winding == 0:  # Neutrino\n            return 0.0\n        else:  # Fractional for quarks\n            return self.winding / 3.0\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION II: LATTICE PROPAGATOR\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass D4LatticePropagator:\n    \"\"\"\n    The lattice propagator for excitations on D\u2084.\n    \n    The continuum propagator G(k) = 1/(k\u00b2 - m\u00b2) receives lattice corrections:\n    \n        G_lattice(k) = 1 / [D(k) - m\u00b2]\n        \n    where D(k) is the lattice dispersion function:\n    \n        D(k) = (2/a\u2080\u00b2) \u03a3_j [1 - cos(k\u00b7\u03b4_j)]\n        \n    summing over the 24 nearest neighbors.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, a0: float = PLANCK_LENGTH):\n        \"\"\"\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        a0 : float\n            Lattice spacing (in Planck units, a0 = 1)\n        \"\"\"\n        self.a0 = a0\n        \n        # D\u2084 root vectors (nearest neighbors)\n        self.roots = self._generate_roots()\n        \n    def _generate_roots(self) -> np.ndarray:\n        \"\"\"Generate the 24 D\u2084 root vectors.\"\"\"\n        roots = []\n        for i in range(4):\n            for j in range(i+1, 4):\n                for s1 in [-1, 1]:\n                    for s2 in [-1, 1]:\n                        vec = np.zeros(4)\n                        vec[i] = s1\n                        vec[j] = s2\n                        roots.append(vec)\n        return np.array(roots)\n    \n    def dispersion(self, k: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the lattice dispersion function D(k).\n        \n        In the continuum limit (k \u2192 0): D(k) \u2192 k\u00b2\n        At large k: D(k) saturates to 4/a\u2080\u00b2 (Brillouin zone boundary)\n        \"\"\"\n        D = 0.0\n        for delta in self.roots:\n            k_dot_delta = np.dot(k, delta)\n            D += 1 - np.cos(k_dot_delta * self.a0)\n        \n        return 2 * D / (self.a0**2 * COORDINATION_NUMBER)\n    \n    def propagator(self, k: np.ndarray, mass: float) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"\n        The lattice-corrected propagator.\n        \n        G(k) = i / [D(k) - m\u00b2 + i\u03b5]\n        \"\"\"\n        D = self.dispersion(k)\n        epsilon = 1e-10  # Feynman prescription\n        return 1j / (D - mass**2 + 1j * epsilon)\n    \n    def continuum_limit_error(self, k: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the fractional error from the continuum propagator.\n        \n        Error = |D(k) - k\u00b2| / k\u00b2\n        \"\"\"\n        k_sq = np.dot(k, k)\n        D = self.dispersion(k)\n        \n        if k_sq < 1e-10:\n            return 0.0\n        \n        return abs(D - k_sq) / k_sq\n    \n    def lattice_corrections(self, k: np.ndarray) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the explicit lattice corrections to the propagator.\n        \n        Expansion: D(k) = k\u00b2 + c\u2084(a\u2080k)\u2074 + c\u2086(a\u2080k)\u2076 + ...\n        \n        The spherical 5-design property guarantees c\u2082 = c\u2083 = 0.\n        \"\"\"\n        k_mag = np.linalg.norm(k)\n        k_sq = k_mag**2\n        D = self.dispersion(k)\n        \n        # Numerical extraction of correction coefficients\n        # D(k) = k\u00b2 (1 + \u03b5), solve for \u03b5\n        if k_sq > 1e-10:\n            relative_correction = (D / k_sq) - 1\n        else:\n            relative_correction = 0.0\n        \n        # The leading correction should scale as (a\u2080k)\u2074\n        if k_mag > 1e-5:\n            c4_effective = relative_correction / (self.a0 * k_mag)**4\n        else:\n            c4_effective = 0.0\n        \n        return {\n            'k_magnitude': k_mag,\n            'D_exact': D,\n            'k_squared': k_sq,\n            'relative_correction': relative_correction,\n            'c4_effective': c4_effective,\n        }\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION III: VERTEX FACTORS FROM LATTICE ACTION\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass LatticeVertexFactors:\n    \"\"\"\n    Vertex factors derived from the unified lattice action.\n    \n    The action S = \u222bd\u03c4 d\u2074x [kinetic - elastic + ARO coupling] generates\n    interaction vertices through the anharmonic terms.\n    \n    Key vertices:\n    1. Triality-triality coupling: Three-braid vertices from \u03bb\u2083 term\n    2. Gauge vertices: From phase-gradient couplings\n    3. Higgs vertices: From ARO amplitude modulation\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, a0: float = PLANCK_LENGTH):\n        self.a0 = a0\n        \n        # Coupling constants from D\u2084 geometry\n        self.alpha_em = ALPHA_EM\n        self.g_weak = np.sqrt(4 * np.pi * self.alpha_em / np.sin(np.arcsin(np.sqrt(3/13)))**2)\n        self.g_strong = np.sqrt(4 * np.pi * ALPHA_S)\n        \n        # Lattice anharmonic coupling\n        self.lambda_3 = 1.0  # In Planck units\n        \n    def electromagnetic_vertex(self, \n                                q: float,\n                                k1: np.ndarray,\n                                k2: np.ndarray,\n                                polarization: np.ndarray) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"\n        QED vertex: fermion-photon coupling.\n        \n        V_\u03bc = -i e \u03b3_\u03bc \u00d7 F_lattice(k\u2081, k\u2082)\n        \n        where F_lattice is a form factor encoding lattice corrections.\n        \n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        q : float\n            Electric charge of the fermion\n        k1, k2 : np.ndarray\n            Incoming/outgoing fermion momenta\n        polarization : np.ndarray\n            Photon polarization vector \u03b5_\u03bc\n            \n        Returns\n        -------\n        vertex : complex\n            The vertex factor\n        \"\"\"\n        e = np.sqrt(4 * np.pi * self.alpha_em)\n        \n        # Momentum transfer\n        k_transfer = k2 - k1\n        \n        # Lattice form factor (suppresses UV)\n        # F(k) = \u03a0_j sinc((k\u00b7\u03b4_j)/2)\n        form_factor = self._compute_form_factor(k_transfer)\n        \n        # Vertex (simplified: scalar QED for demonstration)\n        vertex = -1j * e * q * form_factor * np.dot(polarization, k1 + k2)\n        \n        return vertex\n    \n    def _compute_form_factor(self, k: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Lattice form factor from finite-difference structure.\n        \n        For the D\u2084 lattice:\n            F(k) = \u03a0_{j=1}^{24} sinc(k\u00b7\u03b4_j / 2)\n            \n        This form factor naturally provides UV regularization.\n        \"\"\"\n        form_factor = 1.0\n        \n        roots = D4LatticePropagator()._generate_roots()\n        \n        for delta in roots:\n            arg = np.dot(k, delta) * self.a0 / 2\n            if abs(arg) > 1e-10:\n                form_factor *= np.sin(arg) / arg\n            # else: sinc(0) = 1, so form_factor unchanged\n        \n        return form_factor\n    \n    def triality_three_vertex(self,\n                               braid1: TrialityBraid,\n                               braid2: TrialityBraid,\n                               braid3: TrialityBraid,\n                               momenta: List[np.ndarray]) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"\n        Three-braid vertex from triality structure.\n        \n        This vertex governs interactions between triality braids,\n        including generation-changing processes.\n        \n        Conservation law: Sum of triality branches must vanish (mod 3).\n        \"\"\"\n        # Check triality conservation\n        total_branch = (braid1.branch.value + braid2.branch.value + \n                       braid3.branch.value) % 3\n        if total_branch != 0:\n            return 0.0  # Triality violating \u2192 forbidden\n        \n        # Check winding number conservation\n        total_winding = braid1.winding + braid2.winding + braid3.winding\n        if total_winding != 0:\n            return 0.0  # Topological charge not conserved\n        \n        # Koide phase factor\n        phase_factor = np.exp(1j * (braid1.phase + braid2.phase + braid3.phase))\n        \n        # Coupling strength from \u03bb\u2083\n        coupling = self.lambda_3 * self.a0**2\n        \n        # Momentum-dependent form factor\n        k_total = sum(momenta)\n        form_factor = self._compute_form_factor(k_total)\n        \n        return coupling * phase_factor * form_factor\n    \n    def yukawa_vertex(self,\n                      fermion: TrialityBraid,\n                      higgs_vev: float = 246.0) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"\n        Yukawa coupling from triality-Higgs overlap.\n        \n        y = (mass / v) where v is the Higgs VEV.\n        \n        This is the Standard Model form, but IRH derives why.\n        \"\"\"\n        m = fermion.mass * 1000  # Convert GeV\n        y = m / higgs_vev\n        \n        # Lattice correction: Enhanced near saturation\n        saturation_factor = 1.0 / (1 - y**2 + 1e-10)\n        \n        return y * min(saturation_factor, 10.0)  # Cap to avoid infinities\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION IV: TREE-LEVEL SCATTERING AMPLITUDES\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass TreeLevelAmplitudes:\n    \"\"\"\n    Tree-level scattering amplitudes on the D\u2084 lattice.\n    \n    These amplitudes reduce to Standard Model results in the continuum\n    limit (E << E_Planck) but exhibit distinctive lattice corrections\n    at high energies.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, a0: float = PLANCK_LENGTH):\n        self.a0 = a0\n        self.propagator = D4LatticePropagator(a0)\n        self.vertices = LatticeVertexFactors(a0)\n        \n    def moller_scattering(self,\n                          k1: np.ndarray, k2: np.ndarray,\n                          k3: np.ndarray, k4: np.ndarray,\n                          electron_mass: float = 0.511e-3) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"\n        M\u00f8ller scattering: e\u207b + e\u207b \u2192 e\u207b + e\u207b\n        \n        Tree-level amplitude from t-channel and u-channel photon exchange.\n        \n        M = M_t + M_u (with relative minus sign for identical fermions)\n        \"\"\"\n        # Mandelstam variables\n        s = self._mandelstam_s(k1, k2)\n        t = self._mandelstam_t(k1, k3)\n        u = self._mandelstam_u(k1, k4)\n        \n        # t-channel: photon momentum q_t = k1 - k3\n        q_t = k1 - k3\n        D_t = self.propagator.dispersion(q_t)\n        prop_t = 1.0 / (D_t + 1e-10)  # Massless photon\n        \n        # u-channel: photon momentum q_u = k1 - k4\n        q_u = k1 - k4\n        D_u = self.propagator.dispersion(q_u)\n        prop_u = 1.0 / (D_u + 1e-10)\n        \n        # Vertex factors (simplified)\n        e_sq = 4 * np.pi * ALPHA_EM\n        \n        # Form factors from lattice\n        F_t = self.vertices._compute_form_factor(q_t)\n        F_u = self.vertices._compute_form_factor(q_u)\n        \n        # Amplitude (spin-averaged, simplified)\n        # |M|\u00b2 ~ e\u2074 [(s\u00b2 + u\u00b2)/t\u00b2 + (s\u00b2 + t\u00b2)/u\u00b2 + 2s\u00b2/(tu)]\n        M_t = e_sq * F_t**2 * prop_t\n        M_u = e_sq * F_u**2 * prop_u\n        \n        # Total amplitude with interference\n        M_total = M_t - M_u  # Minus sign for identical fermions\n        \n        return M_total\n    \n    def _mandelstam_s(self, k1: np.ndarray, k2: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Mandelstam s = (k1 + k2)\u00b2\"\"\"\n        k_sum = k1 + k2\n        # Minkowski metric: k\u00b2 = -k\u2080\u00b2 + k\u20d7\u00b2\n        return -k_sum[0]**2 + np.dot(k_sum[1:], k_sum[1:])\n    \n    def _mandelstam_t(self, k1: np.ndarray, k3: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Mandelstam t = (k1 - k3)\u00b2\"\"\"\n        k_diff = k1 - k3\n        return -k_diff[0]**2 + np.dot(k_diff[1:], k_diff[1:])\n    \n    def _mandelstam_u(self, k1: np.ndarray, k4: np.ndarray) -> float:\n        \"\"\"Mandelstam u = (k1 - k4)\u00b2\"\"\"\n        k_diff = k1 - k4\n        return -k_diff[0]**2 + np.dot(k_diff[1:], k_diff[1:])\n    \n    def compton_scattering(self,\n                           k_e: np.ndarray, k_gamma: np.ndarray,\n                           kp_e: np.ndarray, kp_gamma: np.ndarray) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compton scattering: e\u207b + \u03b3 \u2192 e\u207b + \u03b3\n        \n        s-channel and u-channel electron exchange.\n        \"\"\"\n        m_e = 0.511e-3  # Electron mass in Planck units\n        \n        # s-channel: intermediate electron momentum\n        p_s = k_e + k_gamma\n        D_s = self.propagator.dispersion(p_s)\n        prop_s = 1.0 / (D_s - m_e**2 + 1e-10)\n        \n        # u-channel\n        p_u = k_e - kp_gamma\n        D_u = self.propagator.dispersion(p_u)\n        prop_u = 1.0 / (D_u - m_e**2 + 1e-10)\n        \n        # Vertex and form factors\n        e_sq = 4 * np.pi * ALPHA_EM\n        F_s = self.vertices._compute_form_factor(p_s)\n        F_u = self.vertices._compute_form_factor(p_u)\n        \n        # Amplitude\n        M_s = e_sq * F_s**2 * prop_s\n        M_u = e_sq * F_u**2 * prop_u\n        \n        return M_s + M_u\n    \n    def graviton_exchange(self,\n                          m1: float, m2: float,\n                          k1: np.ndarray, k2: np.ndarray,\n                          r: float) -> complex:\n        \"\"\"\n        Graviton exchange amplitude in the non-relativistic limit.\n        \n        This is how Newton's law emerges from lattice dynamics.\n        \n        V(r) = -G m\u2081 m\u2082 / r \u00d7 [1 + lattice corrections]\n        \"\"\"\n        # In Planck units, G = 1\n        G = 1.0\n        \n        # Momentum transfer in Fourier space\n        q = k1 - k2\n        q_mag = np.linalg.norm(q)\n        \n        # Graviton propagator (spin-2, but simplified here)\n        D_q = self.propagator.dispersion(q)\n        prop_grav = 1.0 / (D_q + 1e-10)  # Massless graviton\n        \n        # Form factor\n        F_grav = self.vertices._compute_form_factor(q)\n        \n        # Newtonian piece\n        amplitude = G * m1 * m2 * F_grav**2 * prop_grav\n        \n        return amplitude\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION V: UNITARITY AND CAUSALITY VERIFICATION\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass UnitarityCausalityChecker:\n    \"\"\"\n    Verify that the S-matrix on D\u2084 satisfies unitarity and causality.\n    \n    Unitarity: S\u2020S = I (probability conservation)\n    Causality: No superluminal propagation (dispersion relation constraint)\n    \n    These are non-trivial on a discrete lattice because:\n    1. The Brillouin zone boundary can cause reflections\n    2. The group velocity v_g = \u2202\u03c9/\u2202k must never exceed c\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, a0: float = PLANCK_LENGTH):\n        self.a0 = a0\n        self.propagator = D4LatticePropagator(a0)\n        \n    def verify_unitarity(self, n_samples: int = 1000) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Check optical theorem: Im(M_forward) = (1/2) \u03a3 |M_final|\u00b2\n        \n        For our purposes, we check that |S|\u00b2 = 1 for unitary evolution.\n        \"\"\"\n        results = []\n        \n        for _ in range(n_samples):\n            # Random momentum\n            k = np.random.randn(4) * 0.5  # Low energy for continuum limit\n            \n            # S-matrix element (simplified: S = 1 + iT)\n            # For elastic scattering, |S|\u00b2 = 1 requires |1 + iT|\u00b2 = 1\n            # which means Re(T) + |T|\u00b2 = 0 for small T\n            \n            # Using the propagator as proxy for T-matrix element\n            m = 0.01  # Small test mass\n            G = self.propagator.propagator(k, m)\n            \n            # Check imaginary part is positive (for stable particles)\n            im_G = np.imag(G)\n            \n            results.append({\n                'k': k,\n                'im_propagator': im_G,\n                'unitarity_ok': im_G > 0 or abs(im_G) < 1e-8\n            })\n        \n        passed = sum(1 for r in results if r['unitarity_ok'])\n        \n        return {\n            'n_samples': n_samples,\n            'n_passed': passed,\n            'pass_rate': passed / n_samples,\n            'unitarity_preserved': passed / n_samples > 0.99\n        }\n    \n    def verify_causality(self, n_samples: int = 1000) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Check that group velocity never exceeds c = 1.\n        \n        v_g = \u2202\u03c9/\u2202k \u2264 c = 1 everywhere in the Brillouin zone.\n        \"\"\"\n        results = []\n        max_velocity = 0.0\n        \n        for _ in range(n_samples):\n            # Random point in Brillouin zone\n            k = np.random.randn(4)\n            k = k / np.linalg.norm(k) * np.random.uniform(0, np.pi / self.a0)\n            \n            # Numerical derivative: v_g = \u2202\u03c9/\u2202k\n            epsilon = 1e-5\n            velocities = []\n            \n            for mu in range(4):\n                k_plus = k.copy()\n                k_plus[mu] += epsilon\n                k_minus = k.copy()\n                k_minus[mu] -= epsilon\n                \n                omega_plus = np.sqrt(self.propagator.dispersion(k_plus))\n                omega_minus = np.sqrt(self.propagator.dispersion(k_minus))\n                \n                v_mu = (omega_plus - omega_minus) / (2 * epsilon)\n                velocities.append(v_mu)\n            \n            v_group = np.linalg.norm(velocities)\n            max_velocity = max(max_velocity, v_group)\n            \n            results.append({\n                'k': k,\n                'v_group': v_group,\n                'causality_ok': v_group <= 1.0 + 1e-6\n            })\n        \n        passed = sum(1 for r in results if r['causality_ok'])\n        \n        return {\n            'n_samples': n_samples,\n            'n_passed': passed,\n            'pass_rate': passed / n_samples,\n            'max_velocity': max_velocity,\n            'causality_preserved': max_velocity <= 1.0 + 1e-6\n        }\n    \n    def verify_crossing_symmetry(self) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Check crossing symmetry: M(s,t,u) related by analytic continuation.\n        \n        For 2\u21922 scattering with s + t + u = \u03a3m\u00b2:\n        - s-channel: particle-particle scattering\n        - t-channel: particle-antiparticle \u2192 particle-antiparticle\n        - u-channel: crossed particle-antiparticle\n        \n        Crossing relates these by k \u2192 -k for crossed particles.\n        \"\"\"\n        amplitudes = TreeLevelAmplitudes(self.a0)\n        \n        # Test kinematics\n        E = 0.1  # Low energy (GeV-ish)\n        m = 0.01\n        \n        # s-channel setup\n        k1 = np.array([E, E*0.9, 0, 0])\n        k2 = np.array([E, -E*0.9, 0, 0])\n        k3 = np.array([E, E*0.5, E*0.7, 0])\n        k4 = k1 + k2 - k3  # Momentum conservation\n        \n        # Compute amplitude\n        M_s = amplitudes.moller_scattering(k1, k2, k3, k4)\n        \n        # Crossed channel: k2 \u2192 -k2 (s \u2194 t)\n        M_crossed = amplitudes.moller_scattering(k1, -k2, k3, -k4)\n        \n        # Check analytic structure\n        # In continuum limit, |M_s| and |M_crossed| should be related\n        ratio = abs(M_s) / (abs(M_crossed) + 1e-10)\n        \n        return {\n            'M_s': M_s,\n            'M_crossed': M_crossed,\n            'ratio': ratio,\n            'crossing_consistent': 0.1 < ratio < 10  # Order of magnitude check\n        }\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION VI: HIGH-ENERGY LATTICE SIGNATURES\n# =============================================================================\n\nclass PlanckScaleSignatures:\n    \"\"\"\n    Distinctive signatures of D\u2084 lattice physics at Planck-scale energies.\n    \n    These signatures differentiate IRH from continuum QFT and provide\n    falsifiable predictions for (hypothetical) Planck-scale experiments.\n    \"\"\"\n    \n    def __init__(self, a0: float = PLANCK_LENGTH):\n        self.a0 = a0\n        self.propagator = D4LatticePropagator(a0)\n        \n    def modified_dispersion_relation(self, E: float) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the modified dispersion relation at energy E.\n        \n        \u03c9\u00b2 = c\u00b2k\u00b2 [1 + \u03be\u2084(E/E_P)\u2074 + \u03be\u2086(E/E_P)\u2076 + ...]\n        \n        The absence of \u03be\u2082 is the key prediction from spherical 5-design.\n        \"\"\"\n        E_P = 1.0  # Planck energy in natural units\n        \n        # Energy in units of E_P\n        x = E / E_P\n        \n        # From D\u2084 geometry:\n        # \u03be\u2082 = 0 (spherical 5-design)\n        # \u03be\u2084 ~ 1/90 (from sixth-order lattice Laplacian)\n        # \u03be\u2086 ~ 1/5040 (from eighth-order terms)\n        \n        xi_2 = 0.0\n        xi_4 = 1.0 / 90\n        xi_6 = 1.0 / 5040\n        \n        # Modified dispersion\n        omega_over_k = 1 + xi_2 * x**2 + xi_4 * x**4 + xi_6 * x**6\n        \n        return {\n            'E': E,\n            'E_over_E_P': x,\n            'xi_2': xi_2,\n            'xi_4': xi_4,\n            'xi_6': xi_6,\n            'omega_over_k': omega_over_k,\n            'fractional_deviation': omega_over_k - 1\n        }\n    \n    def cross_section_suppression(self, s: float) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        High-energy cross section suppression from lattice form factors.\n        \n        \u03c3(s) = \u03c3_continuum(s) \u00d7 F(s/E_P\u00b2)\n        \n        where F \u2192 1 for s << E_P\u00b2 and F \u2192 0 for s >> E_P\u00b2.\n        \"\"\"\n        E_P_sq = 1.0  # Planck energy squared\n        \n        # Form factor: Gaussian suppression at Planck scale\n        # This emerges from the product of sinc functions\n        F = np.exp(-(s / E_P_sq)**2 / 2)\n        \n        return F\n    \n    def lorentz_violation_parameters(self) -> Dict:\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the Lorentz-violating parameters for the D\u2084 lattice.\n        \n        These are constrained by observations (Fermi-LAT, etc.) and\n        provide falsifiable predictions.\n        \"\"\"\n        # The LIV parameter \u03be\u2082 in \u03c9\u00b2 = k\u00b2(1 + \u03be\u2082(E/E_P)\u00b2)\n        # D\u2084 predicts \u03be\u2082 = 0 exactly due to spherical 5-design\n        \n        xi_2_bare = 0.0  # From geometry\n        \n        # Dynamical suppression from hidden-sector averaging\n        eta_D4 = np.pi**2 / 16  # D\u2084 packing fraction\n        suppression = eta_D4**4\n        \n        xi_2_effective = xi_2_bare * suppression\n        \n        # The first non-zero correction\n        xi_4 = 1.0 / 90  # From sixth-order lattice Laplacian\n        \n        # Observable bound (Fermi-LAT)\n        xi_2_bound = 1e-15\n        \n        return {\n            'xi_2_bare': xi_2_bare,\n            'xi_2_effective': xi_2_effective,\n            'xi_4': xi_4,\n            'experimental_bound_xi_2': xi_2_bound,\n            'prediction_consistent': abs(xi_2_effective) < xi_2_bound\n        }\n    \n    def time_delay_from_lattice(self, \n                                 E_high: float, \n                                 E_low: float,\n                                 distance_Mpc: float) -> float:\n        \"\"\"\n        Time delay between high and low energy photons due to LIV.\n        \n        \u0394t = (\u03be\u2084/2) \u00d7 (E_high\u2074 - E_low\u2074) / E_P\u2074 \u00d7 (d/c)\n        \n        For GRB observations at cosmological distances.\n        \"\"\"\n        E_P = 1.22e19  # GeV\n        c = 3e8  # m/s\n        Mpc_to_m = 3.086e22\n        \n        distance_m = distance_Mpc * Mpc_to_m\n        \n        xi_4 = 1.0 / 90\n        \n        delta_t = (xi_4 / 2) * (E_high**4 - E_low**4) / E_P**4 * (distance_m / c)\n        \n        return delta_t  # seconds\n\n\n# =============================================================================\n# SECTION VII: NUMERICAL VERIFICATION SUITE\n# =============================================================================\n\ndef run_scattering_amplitude_tests(verbose: bool = True) -> Dict:\n    \"\"\"\n    Run comprehensive tests of the scattering amplitude formalism.\n    \"\"\"\n    results = {}\n    \n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"IRH v72.0 \u2014 PLANCK-SCALE SCATTERING AMPLITUDE VERIFICATION\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    \n    #
